At loader entry, against every static id in the manifest. No filesystem access, no\n    logging, no globals touched — so callers can apply it freely on\n    the hot path without worrying about side effects.\n\n    Args:\n        eval_id: The eval identifier to check. Schema-valid manifests\n            already constrain this to a string at the schema layer, but\n            the runtime guard does its own type check so callers that\n            bypass the schema (e.g., post-parameterize expansion code\n            paths) cannot accidentally smuggle non-string scalars\n            through.\n\n    Raises:\n        InvalidEvalId: Raised when ``eval_id`` is not a ``str`` or when\n            it does not match :data:`EVAL_ID_REGEX`. The exception\n            carries the offending value on :attr:`InvalidEvalId.eval_id`\n            and MUST be mapped by the CLI boundary to\n            :data:`INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE` (= 2).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    # Type guard first so YAML scalars that decode to int/bool/None cannot\n    # bypass the regex check via duck typing.\n    if not isinstance(eval_id, str):\n        raise InvalidEvalId(eval_id)\n\n    if EVAL_ID_REGEX.fullmatch(eval_id) is None:\n        raise InvalidEvalId(eval_id)\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass _Violation:\n    \"\"\"One jsonschema error projected into a stable, log-friendly shape.\"\"\"\n\n    path: str\n    message: str\n\n    def render(self) -> str:\n        return f\"{self.path}: {self.message}\"\n\n\nclass SchemaError(Exception):\n    \"\"\"Raised when a manifest fails ``suite.schema.json`` validation.\n\n    The exception carries the manifest path, every collected\n    :class:`jsonschema.exceptions.ValidationError` projected into a\n    field-path / message pair, and a pre-rendered multi-line ``message``\n    suitable for stderr. The CLI maps this to exit code\n    :data:`SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE` (= 2).\n\n    Attributes:\n        manifest_path: Filesystem path of the manifest that failed.\n        violations: Tuple of ``(json_path, error_message)`` pairs, one per\n            jsonschema error. Order is deterministic (sorted by JSON path)\n            so reporter output is stable across runs.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        manifest_path: Path,\n        violations: Iterable[_Violation],\n    ) -> None:\n        self.manifest_path = manifest_path\n        self.violations: tuple[tuple[str, str], ...] = tuple(\n            (v.path, v.message) for v in violations\n        )\n        lines = [f\"Manifest schema validation failed: {manifest_path}\"]\n        lines.extend(f\"  - {path}: {msg}\" for path, msg in self.violations)\n        super().__init__(\"\\n\".join(lines))\n\n\ndef _format_json_path(error: ValidationError) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Render a jsonschema error path as ``$.evals[0].id`` notation.\n\n    ``$`` denotes the manifest root so the top-level key for a missing\n    required field is also reportable (jsonschema leaves ``absolute_path``\n    empty in that case and stores the field name in ``message``).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    if not error.absolute_path:\n        return \"$\"\n    parts: list[str] = [\"$\"]\n    for segment in error.absolute_path:\n        if isinstance(segment, int):\n            parts.append(f\"[{segment}]\")\n        else:\n            parts.append(f\".{segment}\")\n    return \"\".join(parts)\n\n\ndef _load_schema() -> Mapping[str, Any]:\n    return yaml.safe_load(SCHEMA_PATH.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n\n\ndef _read_manifest(path: Path) -> Mapping[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Decode the YAML manifest at ``path``.\n\n    The read is a pure ``open(...).read()`` — no scratch directories or\n    artifact paths are touched. YAML decode errors surface as\n    :class:`SchemaError` so CLI callers only have to handle a single\n    typed error to honour FR-SCH1.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    try:\n        raw = path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    except FileNotFoundError as exc:\n        violation = _Violation(path=\"$\", message=f\"manifest file not found: {exc}\")\n        raise SchemaError(path, (violation,)) from exc\n    except OSError as exc:\n        violation = _Violation(path=\"$\", message=f\"manifest read failed: {exc}\")\n        raise SchemaError(path, (violation,)) from exc\n\n    try:\n        decoded = yaml.safe_load(raw)\n    except yaml.YAMLError as exc:\n        violation = _Violation(path=\"$\", message=f\"YAML decode failed: {exc}\")\n        raise SchemaError(path, (violation,)) from exc\n\n    if not isinstance(decoded, Mapping):\n        violation = _Violation(\n            path=\"$\",\n            message=(\n                \"manifest must decode to a mapping at the top level; \"\n                f\"got {type(decoded).__name__}\"\n            ),\n        )\n        raise SchemaError(path, (violation,))\n\n    return decoded\n\n\ndef _validate_manifest_dict(path: Path) -> Mapping[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Read ``path``, decode YAML, and validate against ``suite.schema.json``.\n\n    Returns the raw manifest mapping (suite envelope + ``evals[]``) so\n    callers that need suite-level metadata (``name``, ``version``,\n    ``required_binaries``, etc.) — notably :class:`SuiteLoader` — do not\n    have to re-read or re-parse the file. This is the single ingress\n    point for FR-SCH1: every schema rejection raised inside the loader\n    flows through here.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    manifest = _read_manifest(path)\n\n    schema = _load_schema()\n    validator = Draft202012Validator(schema)\n    errors = sorted(\n        validator.iter_errors(manifest),\n        key=lambda e: (list(e.absolute_path), e.message),\n    )\n    if errors:\n        violations = [\n            _Violation(path=_format_json_path(err), message=err.message)\n            for err in errors\n        ]\n        raise SchemaError(path, violations)\n\n    return manifest\n\n\ndef validate_manifest(path: Path | str) -> list[EvalSpec]:\n    \"\"\"Validate ``path`` against ``suite.schema.json`` and return parsed evals.\n\n    Args:\n        path: Filesystem path to a ``suites/*.yaml`` manifest. ``str`` is\n            accepted for ergonomics; the value is normalised to ``Path``.\n\n    Returns:\n        A list of :class:`EvalSpec` instances, one per ``evals[]`` entry\n        in the manifest. Order matches the manifest order so downstream\n        report ordering is deterministic.\n\n    Raises:\n        SchemaError: Raised when the manifest cannot be read, cannot be\n            decoded as YAML, decodes to a non-mapping, or violates any\n            constraint in ``suite.schema.json``. The exception carries\n            one violation entry per jsonschema error, each annotated\n            with a ``$.path.to.field`` JSON path. CLI callers MUST map\n            this to :data:`SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE` (= 2).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    manifest_path = Path(path)\n    manifest = _validate_manifest_dict(manifest_path)\n    return [EvalSpec.from_dict(entry) for entry in manifest[\"evals\"]]\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# COMP-002 SuiteLoader (T01.07 / D-0006)\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\nUNRESOLVED_CAPABILITY_EXIT_CODE: int = 2\n\"\"\"Process exit code emitted when :class:`UnresolvedCapability` reaches the CLI.\n\nThe third member of the SuiteLoader error trio (alongside\n:data:`SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE` and :data:`INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE`). All\nthree map to ``2`` per the design-spec §4 exit-code table so operators see\na single \"harness rejected the manifest before any filesystem write\"\noutcome regardless of which gate fired.\n\"\"\"\n\n\nSUITE_LOADER_ERROR_EXIT_CODE: int = 2\n\"\"\"Single canonical exit code for every :class:`SuiteLoaderError` subclass.\n\n``SuiteLoader`` only ever exits with ``2`` because every loader-layer\nfailure is, from the operator's point of view, \"the harness refused to\nstart running the suite\". The per-class constants\n(:data:`SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE`, :data:`INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE`,\n:data:`UNRESOLVED_CAPABILITY_EXIT_CODE`) are kept so callers can branch on\nintent without coupling the failure classes; this constant is the\naggregate ``SuiteLoader``-boundary alias used by CLI tests.\n\"\"\"\n\n\nclass UnresolvedCapability(Exception):\n    \"\"\"Raised when a required capability cannot be satisfied at load time.\n\n    Surfaced by :class:`SuiteLoader` after :func:`validate_eval_id` has\n    accepted every static id but before parameterize expansion runs. The\n    exception names the capabilities that failed so reporters can render\n    them verbatim; CLI callers MUST map this to\n    :data:`UNRESOLVED_CAPABILITY_EXIT_CODE` (= 2).\n\n    Attributes:\n        eval_id: The eval whose ``requires`` clause could not be\n            satisfied.\n        missing: Tuple of capability names that the injected\n            :class:`CapabilityResolver` rejected.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, eval_id: str, missing: Iterable[str]) -> None:\n        self.eval_id = eval_id\n        self.missing: tuple[str, ...] = tuple(missing)\n        missing_list = \", \".join(self.missing) if self.missing else \"<none>\"\n        super().__init__(\n            f\"eval {eval_id!r} requires unresolved capabilities: {missing_list}\"\n        )\n\n\n# Convenience alias so callers can catch any loader-layer failure with a\n# single ``except SuiteLoaderError`` clause. We intentionally do not make\n# the three concrete classes inherit from a new base — the per-class\n# subclassing already keeps them disjoint, and a base class would force\n# downstream callers (and existing tests) to deal with a new ABC.\nSuiteLoaderError = (SchemaError, InvalidEvalId, UnresolvedCapability)\n\n\n@runtime_checkable\nclass CapabilityResolver(Protocol):\n    \"\"\"Pluggable contract the SuiteLoader uses to gate ``requires`` clauses.\n\n    The Protocol intentionally has a single method so the M1 SuiteLoader\n    can be wired before COMP-009 ``CapabilityGates`` (T01.11) lands. The\n    real implementation arriving in T01.11 inspects PATH + MCP reachability\n    + ``--no-mcp`` flags and either returns the set of unresolved\n    capability names (which the loader turns into\n    :class:`UnresolvedCapability`) or returns an empty iterable.\n\n    Test code injects a stub that records its calls so the \"id-check\n    happens BEFORE capability resolution\" acceptance criterion can be\n    verified by call-order assertion rather than by side-effects.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def resolve(\n        self,\n        eval_id: str,\n        required: tuple[str, ...],\n    ) -> Iterable[str]:\n        \"\"\"Return the subset of ``required`` that could not be satisfied.\n\n        Implementations MUST be pure with respect to the SuiteLoader\n        invariants: no filesystem writes, no globals touched. Returning\n        an empty iterable means every capability is satisfied; any\n        non-empty iterable is bubbled up by the loader as\n        :class:`UnresolvedCapability`.\n        \"\"\"\n        ...\n\n\nclass PermissiveCapabilityResolver:\n    \"\"\"Default :class:`CapabilityResolver` that approves every requirement.\n\n    Used by :class:`SuiteLoader` when the caller does not inject a real\n    resolver — tests and ``eval doctor``'s dry runs both benefit from a\n    no-op default. The real ``CapabilityGates`` implementation lands in\n    T01.11 and is wired through ``commands.py`` (T01.13).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def resolve(\n        self,\n        eval_id: str,\n        required: tuple[str, ...],\n    ) -> Iterable[str]:\n        return ()\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass ParsedSuite:\n    \"\"\"Schema-validated, capability-checked, parameterize-expanded suite.\n\n    The :class:`SuiteLoader.load` return shape. Every field is a verbatim\n    projection of the manifest envelope except ``evals``, which holds the\n    *expanded* eval list (parameterize rows materialised into one\n    ``EvalSpec`` per row, with ``.{index}``-suffixed ids).\n\n    Sequence fields are tuples so the dataclass is hashable and downstream\n    consumers cannot mutate them through the container.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    name: str\n    version: str\n    description: str\n    defaults: Mapping[str, Any]\n    required_binaries: tuple[Mapping[str, Any], ...]\n    optional_capabilities: tuple[Mapping[str, Any], ...]\n    evals: tuple[EvalSpec, ...]\n    source_path: Path\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass SuiteLoader:\n    \"\"\"COMP-002 orchestrator: turn a manifest path into a :class:`ParsedSuite`.\n\n    The gate ordering is fixed and documented in\n    ``artifacts/D-0006/spec.md`` (T01.07):\n\n    1. **Read + schema** (``_validate_manifest_dict``) — FR-SCH1.\n    2. **Static id regex** (``validate_eval_id`` on every ``evals[].id``) —\n       FR-SCH2 / NFR-SEC1. Runs BEFORE capability resolution so a\n       traversal-pattern id cannot trigger ``shutil.which`` calls or\n       network MCP-reachability probes.\n    3. **Capability resolution** (the injected :class:`CapabilityResolver`)\n       — COMP-009 contract surface.\n    4. **Parameterize expansion** — generate ``E2.1, E2.2, ...`` from\n       ``parameterize:`` rows, base-id suffixed by ``.{index}``.\n    5. **Expanded-id regex re-check** (``validate_eval_id`` again) —\n       the load-bearing FR-SCH2 application that closes the\n       expansion-time path-traversal surface.\n\n    Every typed error (``SchemaError``, ``InvalidEvalId``,\n    ``UnresolvedCapability``) is allowed to propagate as-is so CLI\n    callers map it to :data:`SUITE_LOADER_ERROR_EXIT_CODE` (= 2) and\n    emit the error class name on stderr — operators see *which* gate\n    fired without us collapsing the failure classes.\n\n    The class is intentionally a plain dataclass with one composed\n    field (the resolver) rather than a long ``__init__`` parameter list,\n    so future T01.14 (ExpectDSL) wiring can be added as additional\n    optional fields without breaking call sites.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    capability_resolver: CapabilityResolver = field(\n        default_factory=PermissiveCapabilityResolver\n    )\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # public API\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def load(self, path: Path | str) -> ParsedSuite:\n        \"\"\"Run the five-stage gate chain and return a :class:`ParsedSuite`.\n\n        Args:\n            path: Filesystem path to a ``suites/*.yaml`` manifest. ``str``\n                accepted for ergonomics; normalised to ``Path``.\n\n        Returns:\n            A :class:`ParsedSuite` whose ``evals`` tuple holds the\n            post-parameterize-expansion eval list. Suite-level metadata\n            is verbatim from the manifest envelope so callers do not\n            need to re-read the file to access ``required_binaries``,\n            ``defaults``, etc.\n\n        Raises:\n            SchemaError: Manifest read / YAML decode / jsonschema\n                violation. Exit-mapped via\n                :data:`SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE`.\n            InvalidEvalId: A static or post-expansion eval id failed\n                :func:`validate_eval_id`. Exit-mapped via\n                :data:`INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE`. Raised BEFORE any\n                capability resolution call (verified by mocked test).\n            UnresolvedCapability: The injected\n                :class:`CapabilityResolver` rejected one or more\n                ``requires`` entries. Exit-mapped via\n                :data:`UNRESOLVED_CAPABILITY_EXIT_CODE`.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        manifest_path = Path(path)\n\n        # Stage 1+2: schema and read. Re-uses validate_manifest's\n        # ingress so any FR-SCH1 rejection flows through one code path.\n        manifest = _validate_manifest_dict(manifest_path)\n        raw_evals = manifest[\"evals\"]\n\n        # Stage 3: FR-SCH2 static id guard. Apply to every manifest id\n        # BEFORE we touch the capability resolver — see the spec.md\n        # \"ordering rationale\" section for why this matters\n        # (CapabilityGates can shell out via shutil.which / MCP probes).\n        for entry in raw_evals:\n            validate_eval_id(entry.get(\"id\"))\n\n        # Stage 4: capability resolution. Each eval's ``requires`` list\n        # is funnelled through the injected resolver; the first eval\n        # with unresolved capabilities short-circuits the load.\n        for entry in raw_evals:\n            self._check_capabilities(entry)\n\n        # Stage 5: parameterize expansion + post-expansion id re-check.\n        # Static ids without ``parameterize`` are passed through; ids\n        # with ``parameterize`` produce one expanded EvalSpec per row.\n        expanded: list[EvalSpec] = []\n        for entry in raw_evals:\n            expanded.extend(self._expand_entry(entry))\n\n        return ParsedSuite(\n            name=manifest[\"name\"],\n            version=manifest[\"version\"],\n            description=manifest[\"description\"],\n            defaults=manifest.get(\"defaults\", {}),\n            required_binaries=tuple(manifest.get(\"required_binaries\", ())),\n            optional_capabilities=tuple(manifest.get(\"optional_capabilities\", ())),\n            evals=tuple(expanded),\n            source_path=manifest_path,\n        )\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # internals\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def _check_capabilities(self, entry: Mapping[str, Any]) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Funnel one eval's ``requires`` clause through the resolver.\"\"\"\n\n        required = tuple(entry.get(\"requires\", ()))\n        missing = tuple(self.capability_resolver.resolve(entry[\"id\"], required))\n        if missing:\n            raise UnresolvedCapability(eval_id=entry[\"id\"], missing=missing)\n\n    def _expand_entry(self, entry: Mapping[str, Any]) -> list[EvalSpec]:\n        \"\"\"Materialise ``parameterize`` rows into one ``EvalSpec`` per row.\n\n        Expansion convention (design-spec §5):\n\n        * Base id with no ``parameterize`` → one ``EvalSpec`` with the\n          static id unchanged.\n        * Base id with N ``parameterize`` rows → N ``EvalSpec`` rows\n          whose ids are ``{base}.{1..N}``.\n\n        Each expanded id is re-checked against :func:`validate_eval_id`\n        before it returns — this is the load-bearing FR-SCH2 application\n        that the security model relies on. The current 1-based index\n        convention is safe by construction (digits-only suffix), but\n        the guard is mandatory because future expansion strategies\n        (e.g., named indices) could leak unsafe characters.\n\n        Substitution of ``{{key}}`` tokens inside other fields is the\n        runtime layer's responsibility, not the loader's: the schema\n        already constrained per-row keys to ``^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*$``,\n        so any token a row carries is safe to interpolate later. The\n        loader's job is to preserve the row data on the expanded\n        ``EvalSpec`` (currently round-tripped via ``EvalSpec.from_dict``\n        keeping ``parameterize`` populated) so the runner can apply\n        substitutions per-id.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        parameterize_rows = entry.get(\"parameterize\", ())\n        if not parameterize_rows:\n            return [EvalSpec.from_dict(entry)]\n\n        expanded: list[EvalSpec] = []\n        base_id = entry[\"id\"]\n        for index, _row in enumerate(parameterize_rows, start=1):\n            expanded_id = f\"{base_id}.{index}\"\n            # FR-SCH2 re-check — see method docstring for rationale.\n            validate_eval_id(expanded_id)\n            expanded_entry = dict(entry)\n            expanded_entry[\"id\"] = expanded_id\n            expanded.append(EvalSpec.from_dict(expanded_entry))\n        return expanded\n","numLines":624,"startLine":1,"totalLines":624}}}

D-0011 — `superclaude eval doctor` subcommand spec\n\n**Task:** T01.13 (Phase 1, Roadmap FR-CLI4 / R-011)\n**Module:** `src/superclaude/cli/eval/commands.py`\n**CLI surface:** `superclaude eval doctor [--json] [--no-mcp] [--check-coverage]`\n**Status:** Implemented 2026-05-20\n\n## Command surface\n\n| Flag | Type | Effect |\n|---|---|---|\n| `--json` | bool flag | Emit a JSON payload extending the `CapabilityReport` contract; stdout only. |\n| `--no-mcp` | bool flag | Forwarded to `CapabilityGates(skip_flags={\"--no-mcp\"})` so SOFT-SKIP MCP rows are flag-overridden. |\n| `--check-coverage` | bool flag | Reserved for FR-G5 coverage gate (M4 T04.14); for M1 it stamps a `coverage_gate` marker into the JSON payload and prints a deferral note in the human checklist. |\n\nExit codes:\n\n* `0` — every HARD capability satisfied.\n* `2` (`HARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE`) — at least one HARD capability failed.\n  The stderr artifact (`render_hard_failure_artifact`) lists every\n  offending capability name + description + detail.\n\n## Green-checklist format\n\nRendered by `render_checklist(report)`:\n\n```\nsuperclaude eval doctor:\n  [ok] <description> -- <detail>\n  [--] <description> (SOFT-SKIP) -- <detail>\n  [XX] <description> (HARD) -- <detail>\nskip flags: <…>            # only when CapabilityReport.skip_flags is non-empty\nall HARD capabilities satisfied | HARD failures: <names>\nsoft skips: <names>        # only when there are no HARD failures\n```\n\nRow markers are emitted by `_row_marker(row)` and never use shell colour\ncodes so the output is safe to capture in CI logs.\n\n## JSON payload schema\n\nThe payload is `CapabilityReport.to_json()` plus a single extension key:\n\n```json\n{\n  \"report\":         [ {CapabilityStatus rows…} ],\n  \"blocked_evals\":  [],\n  \"skip_flags\":     [],\n  \"hard_failures\":  [],\n  \"soft_skips\":     [],\n  \"soft_xfails\":    [],\n  \"coverage_gate\": {\n    \"requested\":  bool,\n    \"status\":     \"deferred\",\n    \"milestone\":  \"M4\",\n    \"task\":       \"T04.14\"\n  }\n}\n```\n\n`json.dumps(payload, indent=2, sort_keys=True)` guarantees byte-level\ndeterminism across invocations on a stable host (verified by\n`test_cli_doctor_json_is_deterministic_across_invocations`).\n\nThe `report[]` list ordering is fixed: the seven default capability rows\n(from `_DEFAULT_CAPABILITY_SPECS`) in declaration order, followed by the\nthree doctor-specific rows in this fixed order:\n\n1. `claude.min_version`     — HARD\n2. `filesystem.claude_home` — HARD\n3. `vendored.ptytest`       — SOFT-SKIP\n\n## Supplementary capability rows (T01.13-specific)\n\n`CapabilityGates` covers the binary / MCP roster per `D-0010`. The\ndoctor command appends three rows via `build_doctor_report()`:\n\n| Row name | Failure mode | Probe | Notes |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `claude.min_version` | hard | `claude --version` parsed for `>=0.5.0` | Probe is injectable via `_default_claude_version_probe`; fails closed when binary missing, output unparseable, or below floor. |\n| `filesystem.claude_home` | hard | `Path.home() / \".claude\"` is_dir | Override via `claude_home=` for tests. |\n| `vendored.ptytest` | skip | `<cli/eval>/pty/__init__.py` is_file | SOFT-SKIP until M2 vendoring task;       in this fixed order: ``claude.min_version``,\n        ``filesystem.claude_home``, ``vendored.ptytest``.\n    \"\"\"\n    if gates is None:\n        gates = CapabilityGates(skip_flags=skip_flags or ())\n    base = gates.check_all()\n    extras = (\n        _check_claude_version(probe=claude_version_probe),\n        _check_claude_home(home=claude_home),\n human checklist line.\"\"\"\n    if row.passed:\n        return \"[ok]\", \"\"\n    if row.skipped_by_flag:\n        return \"[--]\", \" (skipped by flag)\"\n    if row.failure_mode == \"hard\":\n        return \"[XX]\", \" (HARD)\"\n    if row.failure_mode == \"skip\":\n        return \"[--]\", \" (SOFT-SKIP)\"\n    return \"[??]\", \" (xfail)\"\n\n\ndef render_checklist(report: CapabilityReport) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Render the doctor green-checklist text (FR-CLI4 human output).\"\"\"\n    lines = [\"superclaude eval doctor:\"]\n    for row in report.report:\n        marker, tag = _row_marker(row)\n        detail = f\" -- {row.detail}\" if row.detail else \"\"\n        lines.append(f\"  {marker} {row.description}{tag}{detail}\")\n    if report.skip_flags:\n        lines.append(f\"skip flags: {', '.join(report.skip_flags)}\")\n    if report.hard_failures:\n        lines.append(f\"HARD failures: {', '.join(report.hard_failures)}\")\n    else:\n        lines.append(\"all HARD capabilities satisfied\")\n        if report.soft_skips:\n            lines.append(f\"soft skips: {', '.join(report.soft_skips)}\")\n    return \"\\n\".join(lines)\n\n\ndef render_hard_failure_artifact(report: CapabilityReport) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Render the stderr HARD-failure artifact per FR-CLI4 AC.\"\"\"\n    by_name = {row.name: row for row in report.report}\n    lines = [\"eval doctor: HARD failures\"]\n    for name in report.hard_failures:\n        row = by_name.get(name)\n        if row is None:\n            lines.append(f\"  - {name}\")\n            continue\n        lines.append(f\"  - {name} ({row.description}): {row.detail}\")\n    return \"\\n\".join(lines)\n\n\ndef doctor_payload(report: CapabilityReport, *, check_coverage: bool) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Build the ``--json`` payload.\n\n    Extends :meth:`CapabilityReport.to_json` with a ``coverage_gate``\n    marker so callers can detect whether ``--check-coverage`` was passed.\n    The base ``CapabilityReport`` keys are preserved verbatim so the\n    payload remains compatible with the D-0010 contract.\n    \"\"\"\n    payload = report.to_json()\n    payload[\"coverage_gate\"] = {\n Defaults to `src/superclaude/cli/eval/suites/` (the package suites directory). |\n\nExit codes:\n\n* `0` — every discovered manifest loaded green, or no manifests were\n  found. The empty-directory case is explicitly part of the contract\n  (FR-CLI2 AC).\n* `2` (`SUITE_LOADER_ERROR_EXIT_CODE`) — at least one manifest failed\n  schema validation, eval-id regex, or capability resolution. The\n  stderr line names the offending manifest and the typed error class\n  (`SchemaError`, `InvalidEvalId`, `UnresolvedCapability`) so operators\n  can locate the failure without digging through stack traces.\n\n## Output schema\n\n### Human (default)\n\nRendered by `render_list_text(summaries)`:\n\n```\nsuperclaude eval list:\n  - <name> (version <version>, <count> eval[s])\n  - <name> (version <version>, <count> eval[s])\n```\n\nThe empty case prints `  (no suites found)`. Pluralisation toggles\nbetween `eval` (1) and `evals` (0 or >=2) so the output reads\nnaturally without losing parseability for downstream awk-style\nconsumers.\n\n### JSON (`--json`)\n\n```json\n[\n  { \"name\": \"<string>\", \"version\": \"<string>\", \"eval_count\": <int> },\n  …\n]\n```\n\n`json.dumps(payload, indent=2, sort_keys=True)` guarantees byte-level\ndeterminism across invocations on a stable host (verified by\n`test_cli_list_json_is_deterministic_across_invocations`).\n\n## Eval count semantics\n\n`eval_count` is the **post-parameterize-expansion** count\n(`len(ParsedSuite.evals)`), not the raw `evals[]` length. This reflects\nthe operator-facing question \"how many evals would run if I executed\nthis suite?\" rather than the manifest's row count. A manifest with one\nstatic row plus one parameterized row of three values reports\n`eval_count: 4`, not `eval_count: 2`.\n\n## Discovery rules\n\n`discover_suite_manifests(suites_dir)` returns the glob\n`suites_dir/*.yaml` sorted by filename:\n\n* Missing or non-directory `suites_dir` → empty list (no error). A\n  fresh checkout that has not yet populated built-in suites must not\n  break `eval list`.\n* Non-YAML files (`README.md`, `suite.schema.json`) are filtered out by\n  the `*.yaml` glob.\n* The sort is alphabetical by filename, providing deterministic output\n  regardless of filesystem iteration order.\n\n## Loader integration\n\n`summarize_suites(suites_dir, *, loader=None)` funnels every discovered\nmanifest through `SuiteLoader.load()` so the same five-stage gate chain\n(schema → static id regex → capability resolution → parameterize\nexpansion → expanded-id regex re-check) that protects `eval run`\ngates `eval list` too. There is no separate fast-path that bypasses\nFR-SCH2 — listing an unsafe manifest fails closed with exit 2.\n\nThe `loader` parameter is injectable for tests that want to stub schema\nor capability gates without touching the file system.\n\n## Test injection seams\n\n| Seam | How tests use it |\n|---|---|\n| `--suites-dir` | Fixture suites are copied into a tmp dir per test; the override avoids polluting the package suites directory. |\n| `summarize_suites(loader=...)` | Inject a stubbed `SuiteLoader` for tests that need full control over schema / regex / capability gates. |\n| `discover_suite_manifests` | Pure function over `Path`; tests call it directly on tmp dirs to verify the sort + filter contracts. |\n\n## Wiring\n\n* `eval_list` is registered on the existing `eval_group` (`@eval_group.command(\"list\")`).\n* `eval_group` is already wired into `superclaude.cli.main` via\n  `main.add_command(eval_group, name=\"eval\")` from T01.13 — no new\n  top-level wiring required.\n\n## Acceptance criteria → implementation map\n\n| AC bullet (T01.21) | Implementation site |\n|---|---|\n| `superclaude eval list` exits 0 with at least one suite present and zero suites present (empty-directory case). | `eval_list` Click handler + `render_list_text` empty branch; covered by `test_cli_list_exits_zero_on_empty_directory`, `test_cli_list_exits_zero_with_default_suites_dir`, `test_cli_list_prints_name_version_eval_count`. |\n| `--json` emits a JSON array with `{name, version, eval_count}` entries. | `list_payload` + `json.dumps(..., sort_keys=True)`; covered by `test_cli_list_json_emits_array_of_summaries`, `test_cli_list_json_empty_directory_returns_empty_array`. |\n| Output is deterministic for a given suite directory (sorted by filename). | `discover_suite_manifests` returns `sorted(...)`; covered by `test_cli_list_json_is_deterministic_across_invocations`, `test_cli_list_output_is_sorted_by_filename`. |\n| `artifacts/D-0018/spec.md` records the output schema. | This file. |\n\n## Out of scope for T01.21\n\n- Recursive manifest discovery — only the top-level `*.yaml` glob is\n  searched. Nested manifests would require a roadmap extension.\n- Filtering by category / capability — FR-CLI2 is enumeration-only;\n  filtering belongs to a future `eval list --filter …` enhancement.\n- Hot-reloading of the suite directory — `summarize_suites` re-reads on\n  every invocation; there is no cache.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":224,"cache_read_input_tokens":147127,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":224,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":59,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available","speed":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"15a94346-c5cd-46d7-84f2-33efdfb4e5be","uuid":"e62305a7-2fcc-45d6-ae3c-8da1dc1e1ce3"}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_013kfb9L2MZL1HL2DZUrYksR","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01HeJxY9r8kEh8zDFh7khQqc","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0018/notes.md","content":"# D-0018 — implementation notes\n\n## Design choices\n\n**Post-expansion eval count.** Two interpretations of \"eval count\" were\ncandidate: raw `evals[]` length vs. post-parameterize-expansion length.\nWe chose post-expansion because the operator-facing question is \"how\nmany evals will run\", and the parameterize block exists precisely so\none manifest row materialises N runs. Documenting the choice in the\nspec means future contributors do not need to reverse-engineer it from\ntest expectations.\n\n**SuiteLoader funnels every manifest.** `summarize_suites` calls\n`SuiteLoader.load` for each discovered file rather than a stripped-down\nschema-only reader. This costs a tiny bit of work (a full gate chain\nper manifest) but eliminates the risk of `eval list` exposing a\nmalformed manifest that `eval run` would reject. The two surfaces stay\nin sync by construction.\n\n**`--suites-dir` instead of `--config`.** The roadmap COMP-005\n`EvalConfig` does not yet expose a `suites_dir` field (it ships an\nempty `paths` mapping by default). Rather than retrofit that field\nhere, the flag accepts an explicit directory override. When `EvalConfig`\ngrows a `suites_dir` field in a later milestone, the default lookup\ncan be re-routed through it without breaking the CLI surface.\n\n**Empty-directory case is silent + exit 0.** FR-CLI2 AC explicitly\nrequires the empty case to exit 0. We considered emitting an exit-1\n\"empty suite directory\" diagnostic but rejected it because (a) a fresh\ncheckout legitimately has no built-in suites at M1, and (b) callers\npiping `--json` to `jq` would prefer an empty array to a non-zero exit.\n\n## Things explicitly NOT done\n\n- No filtering by category, capability, or name. FR-CLI2 is\n  enumeration-only.\n- No machine-readable error format for the exit-2 path. The stderr line\n  is intentionally human-readable; future tooling that needs structured\n  errors should consume the JSON payload from `eval doctor` or a\n  dedicated `eval validate` command.\n- No caching. `summarize_suites` re-reads every file on every call.\n\n## Follow-ups\n\n- When `EvalConfig` grows a `suites_dir` field, change\n  `_DEFAULT_SUITES_DIR` to resolve through it.\n- When built-in suites land under `src/superclaude/cli/eval/suites/`,\n  add a non-empty default-listing assertion to the test suite.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":2335,"cache_read_input_tokens":147351,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":2335,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":54,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available","speed":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"15a94346-c5cd-46d7-84f2-33efdfb4e5be","uuid":"6cb06e9b-409c-4f5b-bdb9-a2cb42d80c76"}

Documenting the choice in the\nspec means future contributors do not need to reverse-engineer it from\ntest expectations.\n\n**SuiteLoader funnels every manifest.** `summarize_suites` calls\n`SuiteLoader.load` for each discovered file rather than a stripped-down\nschema-only reader. This costs a tiny bit of work (a full gate chain\nper manifest) but eliminates the risk of `eval list` exposing a\nmalformed manifest that `eval run` would reject. The two surfaces stay\nin sync by construction.\n\n**`--suites-dir` instead of `--config`.** The roadmap COMP-005\n`EvalConfig` does not yet expose a `suites_dir` field (it ships an\nempty `paths` mapping by default). Rather than retrofit that field\nhere, the flag accepts an explicit directory override. When `EvalConfig`\ngrows a `suites_dir` field in a later milestone, the default lookup\ncan be re-routed through it without breaking the CLI surface.\n\n**Empty-directory case is silent + exit 0.** FR-CLI2 AC explicitly\nrequires the empty case to exit 0. We considered emitting an exit-1\n\"empty suite directory\" diagnostic but rejected it because (a) a fresh\ncheckout legitimately has no built-in suites at M1, and (b) callers\npiping `--json` to `jq` would prefer an empty array to a non-zero exit.\n\n## Things explicitly NOT done\n\n- No filtering by category, capability, or name. FR-CLI2 is\n  enumeration-only.\n- No machine-readable error format for the exit-2 path. The stderr line\n  is intentionally human-readable; future tooling that needs structured\n  errors should consume the JSON payload from `eval doctor` or a\n  dedicated `eval validate` command.\n- No caching. `summarize_suites` re-reads every file on every call.\n\n## Follow-ups\n\n- When `EvalConfig` grows a `suites_dir` field, change\n  `_DEFAULT_SUITES_DIR` to resolve through it.\n- When built-in suites land under `src/superclaude/cli/eval/suites/`,\n  add a non-empty default-listing assertion to the test suite.\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null,"userModified":false}}

E1 + E2.1 + E2.2 + E2.3\n    assert summary.source == tmp_path / \"reference.yaml\"\n\n\ndef test_summarize_suites_handles_static_only_manifest(tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n    shutil.copy(\n        FIXTURES_DIR / \"no_parameterize_suite.yaml\",\n        tmp_path / \"static.yaml\",\n    )\n    summaries = summarize_suites(tmp_path)\n    assert summaries[0].name == \"no-parameterize\"\n    assert summaries[0].eval_count == 2  # alpha.yaml ships no-parameterize → name \"no-parameterize\"\n    # zebra.yaml ships reference → name \"reference\"\n    assert [p[\"name\"] for p in payload] == [\"no-parameterize\", \"reference\"]\n\n\ndef test_cli_list_exits_two_on_schema_violation(tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n    shutil.copy(\n        FIXTURES_DIR / \"missing_name_suite.yaml\",\n        tmp_path / \"broken.yaml\",\n    )\n    runner = CliRunner()\n    result = runner.invoke(eval_group, [\"list\", \"--suites-dir\", str(tmp_path)])\n    assert result.exit_code == SUITE_LOADER_ERROR_EXIT_CODE\n    #
